At night, it's just as bad because I lie awake for hours replaying the crash over and over, making it incredibly hard to fall asleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been avoiding talking or thinking about the accident, or have you been trying to discuss it with friends, family, or someone else to process what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been actively avoiding talking or even thinking about it as much as possible. I absolutely do not want to discuss it, and if my coworkers try to bring up the news story, I shut the conversation down immediately because it shakes me up too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling constantly on edge or easily startled since the accident, such as being frightened by sudden noises or having an exaggerated response to surprises?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been incredibly jumpy and on edge since the accident. Even a door closing or someone walking up behind me makes me jump out of my skin. My heart will keep pounding hard for several minutes afterward before I can finally calm down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having difficulty concentrating on your daily tasks or activities since the accident, or has your performance at work or school been affected?\n\nPatient: Yes, my concentration has been completely shot. I find myself staring at spreadsheets unable to focus, which has caused me to miss deadlines, leave early, and make mistakes that my manager actually noticed. I've even had to cancel my mentoring sessions and running club because I just can't focus or face being near busy streets.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a week ago; have they been steadily worsening, staying about the same, or do you feel like you're starting to get a little better in any way since the accident?\n\nPatient: They haven't been easing up or getting better at all, and everything has felt constant and intense since the crash last week. That's actually why I realized I couldn't handle this on my own and decided to reach out for help through my company's employee assistance program.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that these symptoms started about a week ago and have remained constant and intense, but can you tell me if you've experienced any similar symptoms or reactions to stressful events in the past, or if this is the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is completely new to me. I have never experienced anything like this before, and I've certainly never had such a severe or overwhelming reaction to a stressful event in the past."
  }

I've even had to cancel my mentoring sessions and running club because I just can't focus or face being near busy streets.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a week ago; have they been steadily worsening, staying about the same, or do you feel like you're starting to get a little better in any way since the accident?\n\nPatient: They haven't been easing up or getting better at all, and everything has felt constant and intense since the crash last week. That's actually why I realized I couldn't handle this on my own and decided to reach out for help through my company's employee assistance program.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that these symptoms started about a week ago and have remained constant and intense, but can you tell me if you've experienced any similar symptoms or reactions to stressful events in the past, or if this is the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is completely new to me. I have never experienced anything like this before, and I've certainly never had such a severe or overwhelming reaction to a stressful event in the past.\n\nDoctor: Given that your symptoms have been constant and intense since the accident about a week ago, do you think you're starting to have thoughts or feelings that you might not be able to recover from this experience, or that it will have a lasting impact on your life?\n\nPatient: Since it's only been a week, I'm not really sure about the long-term impact yet. But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?"

But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel guilty or blame myself for the accident, as it happened so fast and there was nothing I could have done to stop that truck. I'm mostly just feeling incredibly shaken up, anxious, and completely overwhelmed by the memory of it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of detachment from yourself or the world around you, as if you're observing things from outside your body or as if things aren't real, since the accident?"

No, I haven't really experienced feeling detached from myself or like things aren't real. However, I have noticed some trouble remembering certain parts of the accident itself, like there are big blank spots right after I called 911.

That's actually why I realized I couldn't handle this on my own and decided to reach out for help through my company's employee assistance program.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that these symptoms started about a week ago and have remained constant and intense, but can you tell me if you've experienced any similar symptoms or reactions to stressful events in the past, or if this is the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is completely new to me. But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel guilty or blame myself for the accident, as it happened so fast and there was nothing I could have done to stop that truck. I find myself staring at spreadsheets unable to focus, which has caused me to miss deadlines, leave early, and make mistakes that my manager actually noticed. I've even had to cancel my mentoring sessions and running club because I just can't focus or face being near busy streets.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a week ago; have they been steadily worsening, staying about the same, or do you feel like you're starting to get a little better in any way since the accident?\n\nPatient: They haven't been easing up or getting better at all, and everything has felt constant and intense since the crash last week. That's actually why I realized I couldn't handle this on my own and decided to reach out for help through my company's employee assistance program.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that these symptoms started about a week ago and have remained constant and intense, but can you tell me if you've experienced any similar symptoms or reactions to stressful events in the past, or if this is the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is completely new to me. I have never experienced anything like this before, and I've certainly never had such a severe or overwhelming reaction to a stressful event in the past.\n\nDoctor: Given that your symptoms have been constant and intense since the accident about a week ago, do you think you're starting to have thoughts or feelings that you might not be able to recover from this experience, or that it will have a lasting impact on your life?\n\nPatient: Since it's only been a week, I'm not really sure about the long-term impact yet. But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel guilty or blame myself for the accident, as it happened so fast and there was nothing I could have done to stop that truck. I'm mostly just feeling incredibly shaken up, anxious, and completely overwhelmed by the memory of it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of detachment from yourself or the world around you, as if you're observing things from outside your body or as if things aren't real, since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced feeling detached from myself or like things aren't real. However, I have noticed some trouble remembering certain parts of the accident itself, like there are big blank spots right after I called 911.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these blank spots in your memory of the accident, such as what specifically you don't remember or how often you've noticed them since the accident?\n\nPatient: I vividly remember the screeching tires and seeing the car roll, but there are whole minutes missing right after I called 911. I've tried to piece it together, but there's just a big blank spot where I don't know what I did or said, and it's really frustrating and unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other instances of dissociation, such as feeling like you're in a daze, since the accident, or has it been limited to these memory gaps about the event itself?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just been limited to those memory gaps right after I called 911. I haven't felt like I'm in a daze or disconnected from reality otherwise, just really frustrated by those missing minutes when I try to piece the timeline together.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much time is missing from your memory after calling 911, and have you noticed any triggers that might help you recall those lost minutes?\n\nPatient: I can't really estimate the exact amount of time because of the blank spots, but it feels like whole minutes are completely missing right after I called 911. I haven't found any triggers that help me recall those lost minutes, and they just remain completely blank whenever I try to piece things together."

I have never experienced anything like this before, and I've certainly never had such a severe or overwhelming reaction to a stressful event in the past.\n\nDoctor: Given that your symptoms have been constant and intense since the accident about a week ago, do you think you're starting to have thoughts or feelings that you might not be able to recover from this experience, or that it will have a lasting impact on your life?\n\nPatient: Since it's only been a week, I'm not really sure about the long-term impact yet. But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel guilty or blame myself for the accident, as it happened so fast and there was nothing I could have done to stop that truck. I'm mostly just feeling incredibly shaken up, anxious, and completely overwhelmed by the memory of it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of detachment from yourself or the world around you, as if you're observing things from outside your body or as if things aren't real, since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced feeling detached from myself or like things aren't real. However, I have noticed some trouble remembering certain parts of the accident itself, like there are big blank spots right after I called 911.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these blank spots in your memory of the accident, such as what specifically you don't remember or how often you've noticed them since the accident?\n\nPatient: I vividly remember the screeching tires and seeing the car roll, but there are whole minutes missing right after I called 911. I've tried to piece it together, but there's just a big blank spot where I don't know what I did or said, and it's really frustrating and unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other instances of dissociation, such as feeling like you're in a daze, since the accident, or has it been limited to these memory gaps about the event itself?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just been limited to those memory gaps right after I called 911. I haven't felt like I'm in a daze or disconnected from reality otherwise, just really frustrated by those missing minutes when I try to piece the timeline together.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much time is missing from your memory after calling 911, and have you noticed any triggers that might help you recall those lost minutes?\n\nPatient: I can't really estimate the exact amount of time because of the blank spots, but it feels like whole minutes are completely missing right after I called 911. I haven't found any triggers that help me recall those lost minutes, and they just remain completely blank whenever I try to piece things together.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like pain or discomfort since the accident, or have your blank spots in memory been the primary issue you've been dealing with?\n\nPatient: I wasn't physically injured or in pain because I narrowly avoided being hit, but I do have those distressing blank spots where I remember the crash itself but have missing minutes right after calling 911. While those memory gaps are unsettling, they aren't my only major issue. I'm also struggling heavily with sleep, avoiding driving, and severe anxiety like my chest clamping up."
  }

I've even had to cancel my mentoring sessions and running club because I just can't focus or face being near busy streets.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these symptoms started about a week ago; have they been steadily worsening, staying about the same, or do you feel like you're starting to get a little better in any way since the accident?\n\nPatient: They haven't been easing up or getting better at all, and everything has felt constant and intense since the crash last week. That's actually why I realized I couldn't handle this on my own and decided to reach out for help through my company's employee assistance program.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned that these symptoms started about a week ago and have remained constant and intense, but can you tell me if you've experienced any similar symptoms or reactions to stressful events in the past, or if this is the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this is completely new to me. I have never experienced anything like this before, and I've certainly never had such a severe or overwhelming reaction to a stressful event in the past.\n\nDoctor: Given that your symptoms have been constant and intense since the accident about a week ago, do you think you're starting to have thoughts or feelings that you might not be able to recover from this experience, or that it will have a lasting impact on your life?\n\nPatient: Since it's only been a week, I'm not really sure about the long-term impact yet. But right now, it feels completely overwhelming and I'm really worried because of how much it's already affecting my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of guilt or self-blame related to the accident you witnessed, such as thinking you could have done something to prevent it?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel guilty or blame myself for the accident, as it happened so fast and there was nothing I could have done to stop that truck. I'm mostly just feeling incredibly shaken up, anxious, and completely overwhelmed by the memory of it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of detachment from yourself or the world around you, as if you're observing things from outside your body or as if things aren't real, since the accident?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced feeling detached from myself or like things aren't real. However, I have noticed some trouble remembering certain parts of the accident itself, like there are big blank spots right after I called 911.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these blank spots in your memory of the accident, such as what specifically you don't remember or how often you've noticed them since the accident?\n\nPatient: I vividly remember the screeching tires and seeing the car roll, but there are whole minutes missing right after I called 911. I've tried to piece it together, but there's just a big blank spot where I don't know what I did or said, and it's really frustrating and unsettling.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other instances of dissociation, such as feeling like you're in a daze, since the accident, or has it been limited to these memory gaps about the event itself?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just been limited to those memory gaps right after I called 911. I haven't felt like I'm in a daze or disconnected from reality otherwise, just really frustrated by those missing minutes when I try to piece the timeline together.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much time is missing from your memory after calling 911, and have you noticed any triggers that might help you recall those lost minutes?\n\nPatient: I can't really estimate the exact amount of time because of the blank spots, but it feels like whole minutes are completely missing right after I called 911. I haven't found any triggers that help me recall those lost minutes, and they just remain completely blank whenever I try to piece things together.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like pain or discomfort since the accident, or have your blank spots in memory been the primary issue you've been dealing with?\n\nPatient: I wasn't physically injured or in pain because I narrowly avoided being hit, but I do have those distressing blank spots where I remember the crash itself but have missing minutes right after calling 911. While those memory gaps are unsettling, they aren't my only major issue. I'm also struggling heavily with sleep, avoiding driving, and severe anxiety like my chest clamping up.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned specific memory gaps about the accident, can you tell me if these gaps have been changing or if new memories about the accident have been returning to you since they first started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any changes or any new memories returning at all. Those minutes right after I called 911 still feel completely blank and missing whenever I try to think back to that moment. It's just a total blank spot that hasn't budged since it happened."
  }

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.
